Base directory for this skill: A:\Source\find-local-parks\.claude\skills\bmad-sprint-planning

# Overview

You are a senior developer about to commit to this plan. Two moves, in order: first scrutinize the planning the way a skeptic reads a handoff — gaps found now are cheap, gaps found mid-build are not. Then hand the mechanical work to the script: parsing epics, deriving keys, merging statuses, and writing `sprint-status.yaml` are deterministic jobs, not judgment calls. Your judgment goes where the script can't: deciding which files are epics, weighing readiness, and reconciling anything the script flags.

## On Activation

1. Resolve customization: `uv run {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_customization.py --skill {skill-root} --project-root {project-root} --key workflow`. On failure, read `{skill-root}/customize.toml` directly and use defaults.
2. Execute each entry in `{workflow.activation_steps_prepend}` in order.
3. Treat every entry in `{workflow.persistent_facts}` as foundational context for the rest of the run. Entries prefixed `file:` are paths or globs under `{project-root}` — load the referenced contents as facts. All other entries are facts verbatim.
4. Run `uv run {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_config.py --project-root {project-root}` and resolve `{user_name}`, `{communication_language}`, `{document_output_language}`, `{project_name}`, `{planning_artifacts}`, `{implementation_artifacts}`, `{project_knowledge}` (skip gracefully if unset), and `{date}`. Stay in `{communication_language}` for every turn, not just the greeting.
5. Greet `{user_name}`, detect intent, and load only what that intent needs:
   - **readiness** — check implementation readiness only: load `references/readiness-gate.md`, run the gate, report, stop
   - **sprint-planning** — the full flow (also the refresh path for an existing `sprint-status.yaml`): load `references/readiness-gate.md`, then on PASS `references/generate-tracking.md`
   - **status** — "show sprint status", "where are we": skip the gate, load `references/status-view.md`
   - **validate** — check the tracking file's format: load `references/validate.md`
   - **fix** — repair or rebuild a broken `sprint-status.yaml`: load `references/fix-sprint-status.md`

   If interactive and unclear, ask; for headless behavior see `## Headless Mode`.

Execute each entry in `{workflow.activation_steps_append}` in order.

Activation is complete. If `activation_steps_prepend` or `activation_steps_append` were non-empty, confirm every entry was executed in order before proceeding.

## If the Script Fails

This rule covers every intent: when `sprint_plan.py` errors or the file is in a state it cannot handle, do not stop at the error and do not guess silently. Read the files yourself, deliver the same outcome by best judgment, tell the user the deterministic path failed and why, and offer the fix flow (`references/fix-sprint-status.md`) to restore a file the script can work with.

## On Completion

Whatever the intent, close out in `{communication_language}` per the loaded reference, then run `{workflow.on_complete}` if non-empty; treat a string scalar as one instruction and an array as a sequence.

## Headless Mode

When invoked headless, do not ask. Run the gate and, unless intent was readiness-only, generate tracking. Ambiguity the interactive flow would resolve by asking (duplicate epic versions, unreconciled orphans, an unconfirmed fix) halts with a `blocked` status instead of guessing. End with a JSON response:

```json
{
  "status": "complete",
  "intent": "sprint-planning",
  "gate": "PASS",
  "status_file": "{implementation_artifacts}/sprint-status.yaml",
  "findings": [],
  "warnings": []
}
```

`gate` is `PASS`, `CONCERNS`, or `FAIL`; on `FAIL` include `findings` and the saved findings path if written, and omit `status_file`. `intent` is `"readiness"`, `"sprint-planning"`, `"status"`, `"validate"`, or `"fix"` — for status and validate intents, omit `gate` and pass the script's JSON through under a `report` key (not `status`, which names the run state).

## References

- `scripts/sprint_plan.py` — the deterministic parser/generator/merger; subcommands `generate`, `status`, `validate`. Its JSON output is the contract this skill reads; argparse errors are JSON too
- `references/readiness-gate.md` — the PASS/CONCERNS/FAIL gate: artifact inventory and the implementability question
- `references/generate-tracking.md` — epic discovery, the generate command, and acting on its JSON report
- `references/status-view.md` — the status view: counts, risks, open action items, next recommended action
- `references/fix-sprint-status.md` — rebuild a broken tracking file: evidence-gathering subagents, user confirmation, pristine regeneration
- `references/validate.md` — format validation of an existing `sprint-status.yaml`
- `sprint-status-template.yaml` — the documented file format and status vocabulary; the script embeds the same block and the test suite pins the two copies together

3	Before generating any tracking, judge whether the plan can actually be built. If the user only asked to check readiness, this gate is the deliverable — report the verdict and stop.
4	
5	Inventory what planning actually exists: scan `{planning_artifacts}` and `{project_knowledge}` for intent and planning artifacts — briefs, PRFAQs, PRDs, specs, UX outputs, architecture, epics and stories. Identify documents by reading what they are, not by filename patterns; projects arrive with different artifact mixes and naming.
6	
7	Assess the plan as a whole against one question: **could a developer implement these epics without inventing decisions nothing records?**
8	
9	- Requirements and decisions in the intent artifacts trace forward into stories; stories trace back to recorded intent — flag orphans in both directions
10	- Epics deliver user value and carry no forward dependencies; stories are independently completable
11	- Architecture and UX decisions the stories rely on are recorded somewhere, not assumed
12	- Conflicts between artifacts (a spec and an epic disagreeing) are surfaced, not silently resolved
13	
14	A missing document type is only a finding if stories depend on decisions nothing records — a project with no UX artifact and no UI stories is fine.
15	
16	Deliver a verdict:
17	
18	- **PASS** — state it in one line; for the full sprint-planning intent, continue with `generate-tracking.md`
19	- **CONCERNS** — list them briefly with where each gap lives; ask `{user_name}` whether to proceed anyway or fix first
20	- **FAIL** — the plan is not implementable as recorded. Present findings ordered by severity, name the skill that fixes each (the relevant plan skill, or `bmad-correct-course` for cross-cutting changes), offer to save the findings to `{planning_artifacts}/implementation-readiness.md`, and stop

=== _bmad-output/specs/spec-find-local-parks/SPEC.md
---
id: SPEC-find-local-parks
companions:
  - stack.md
  - park-data.md
  - ../../../design/README.md
sources:
  - ../../../requirements/local-parks-candidate.pdf

> **Canonical contract.** This SPEC and the files in `companions:` are the complete, preservation-validated contract for what to build, test, and validate. Source documents listed in frontmatter are for traceability — consult them only if you need narrative rationale or prose color this contract intentionally omits.

# Find a Park

## Why

A mandate to meet: a 2-hour take-home brief (Granicus Lighthouse) to build a small web app that helps residents find local parks. Evaluation weighs usefulness and accessibility for residents, clear communication of decisions, maintainable code that handles missing data, accountable AI use, and evidence of review and verification. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features.

## Capabilities

- **CAP-1**
  - **intent:** A resident sees where parks are on a map and can get from a marker to that park's details.
  - **success:** Every park with valid lat/lng has a marker; activating a marker opens the park's details or a clear link to them.
- **CAP-2**
  - **intent:** A resident browses all parks in a list and selects one without using the map.
  - **success:** All parks in the data appear, including ones with missing fields; a list item opens details by mouse, touch, and keyboard (Tab to it, Enter to open).
- **CAP-3**
  - **intent:** A resident views the available information for a park: name, address, description, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating.
  - **success:** Each of the 12 sample parks renders; null, missing, or empty fields are omitted or labelled as not listed, never invented; broken image URLs degrade without a broken-image icon or crash.
- **CAP-4**
  - **intent:** The main tasks (find a park, open its details) work on phone and desktop, by keyboard, and with assistive technology.
  - **success:** At 375px width and at desktop width the list→details flow works with no horizontal scroll; every interactive element shows a visible focus indicator; regions and controls have readable text and clear labels.
- **CAP-5**
  - **intent:** The interviewer can run, understand, and judge the submission from its README.
  - **success:** README contains run instructions, what works, what was left out, decisions, assumptions, data/schema changes, known issues, how the result was checked, approximate time spent, and next steps before public use.

## Constraints

- Hard 2-hour box for the whole submission, including testing and README; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.
- Must work without the resident's location.
- No private credentials in the repo; services must be keyless or document how the interviewer supplies a key.
- Data fields may be missing or null and arrays empty; the UI must not crash or fabricate details.
- The list and details view must be a complete alternative to map markers.
- Use the supplied `parks.sample.json`; document any extension of data or schema in the README.
- Unredacted AI conversation logs from every tool used are part of the submission: the developer manually exports the Claude Code transcript before each fresh story context, and exports the Claude Design transcript; the generated designs are committed in `design/`.
- Visual design follows `design/README.md` and `design/screens/`; where they conflict with this SPEC, this SPEC wins.

## Non-goals

- Backend, accounts, hosted deployment.
- Search, filters, current-location support — only if the core is done with time remaining.
- Editing or contributing park data.

## Success signal

- On a phone-width viewport and on desktop, a resident can open any of the 12 sample parks from the list using only the keyboard, and from a map marker by click/tap, and see its details with missing fields handled cleanly; the interviewer runs it from the README with one install and one start command.

## Assumptions

- Municipality is the NYC-area set implied by the sample coordinates; the app is titled generically ("Find a Park").
- Parks without valid lat/lng still appear in the list and details, just without a marker.
- A data-load failure shows an error message, not a blank screen.
=== _bmad-output/specs/spec-find-local-parks/park-data.md
# Park data

Source: `requirements/parks.sample.json` — an array of 12 park objects.

## Shape

| Field | Type | May be missing/null/empty |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string (slug) | treat as required |
| `name` | string | treat as required |
| `description` | string | yes |
| `location.lat`, `location.lng` | number | yes (no marker if absent) |
| `location.address` | string | yes |
| `amenities` | string[] (kebab-case tokens, e.g. `dog-run`) | yes / empty |
| `hours` | free-text string (e.g. `Dawn to dusk`, `24 hours`) | yes |
| `images` | string[] URLs | yes / empty |
| `acreage` | number | yes |
| `rating` | number (0–5) | yes |

Amenity tokens display as readable labels (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens fall back to the token humanized, never dropped.

## Image URL transformation

`parks.sample.json` is never edited. At load time each image URL is mapped by filename stem to a bundled asset: `https://images.example.com/prospect-1.jpg` → `src/assets/park-images/prospect-1.png` (source art: `design/park-images/`). A URL with no matching asset passes through unchanged, so the `onError` fallback still applies. All 12 sample URLs have a match. Describe this transformation in the README.

## Known gaps in the sample (test fixtures)

| Park | Gap |
|---|---|
| `old-mill-botanical-garden` | `description: null` |
| `cedar-hill-nature-preserve` | `images: []`, `rating: null` |
| `east-ridge-trailhead` | `images: []` |
| `highland-dog-park` | `location.address` missing |
| all | image URLs are `images.example.com` placeholders that do not resolve; the transformation above maps them to bundled art, and an unmapped URL must still fall back cleanly |
=== _bmad-output/specs/spec-find-local-parks/stack.md
# Stack

All choices confirmed by the user.

| Concern | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| UI | React | User decision. |
| Build tool | Vite | Fast scaffold; one `npm install` + `npm run dev`. |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS, mobile-first | User decision; responsive by default. |
| Map | `react-leaflet` + Leaflet | User decision; no API key. |
| Tiles | OpenStreetMap standard tiles, with attribution | Keyless; satisfies the no-credentials constraint. |
| Data | Static import of `parks.sample.json` (copied into the app) | No backend. |
| Routing | Path-based `/parks/:id` for park details; `/` is the list | Linkable details, working Back. Supersedes the `?park=` option in `design/README.md`. |
| Tests | Vitest + React Testing Library | Cover every known gap in `park-data.md` (null description, empty images, null rating, missing address, failing image URLs) plus the keyboard list→details flow. |

## Conventions

- Map initial view fits the bounds of parks that have coordinates; never depends on geolocation.
- Leaflet's default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite, or markers render as broken images.
- Visual design, tokens, missing-data display copy, and accessibility rules come from `design/README.md` (adopted companion) and `design/screens/`.
- Park images: pixel art lives in `src/assets/park-images/` (moved from `design/park-images/`) and is resolved via the load-time URL transformation in `park-data.md`; the image-failure fallback is still required.
=== _bmad-output/specs/spec-find-local-parks/stories.yaml
- id: "1"
  title: Scaffold app and park data layer
  description: >-
    Vite + React + Tailwind app with a null-safe module that loads and normalizes
    parks.sample.json per park-data.md, a layout shell with list, map, and details regions,
    routing at /parks/:id, and bundled park images resolved by a load-time URL transformation.
    Data-load failure shows an error, not a blank screen.
  spec_checkpoint: true
  done_checkpoint: true
  invoke_dev_with: >-
    Read stack.md and park-data.md. Copy requirements/parks.sample.json into the app unchanged.
    Move design/park-images/*.png into src/assets/park-images/. Keep the app runnable with one

=== _bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md

project: find-local-parks

# Find a Park: Epics and Stories

Derived from `SPEC-find-local-parks` and its companions. Every story must also be read with:

- `../specs/spec-find-local-parks/SPEC.md`: capabilities CAP-1 to CAP-5, constraints, non-goals
- `../specs/spec-find-local-parks/stack.md`: stack and conventions
- `../specs/spec-find-local-parks/park-data.md`: data shape, the image URL transformation, known gaps
- `../../design/README.md` and `../../design/screens/`: layout, tokens, missing-data wording, accessibility rules

Where the design and the SPEC disagree, the SPEC wins.

## Requirements Coverage

| Capability | Stories |
|---|---|
| CAP-1 Map | 1.4 |
| CAP-2 List | 1.2 |
| CAP-3 Details and missing data | 1.1 (data layer), 1.3 |
| CAP-4 Accessible, responsive UI | 1.2, 1.3, 1.4 (baseline), 1.5 (full pass) |
| CAP-5 README | 1.6 |

Cross-cutting constraints that apply to every story: no geolocation, no credentials, never invent missing data, `parks.sample.json` is never edited, keyboard focus is always visible.

## Epic 1: Find a Park

A resident can find a local park on a map or in a list and open its details on a phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer can run and judge the result from the README.

### Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer

As a developer,
I want a running Vite + React + Tailwind app with a safe park data layer, routing, and bundled images,
So that every later story builds on data that cannot crash the UI.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** a fresh clone
**When** the developer runs one install command and one start command
**Then** the app starts locally with no API keys or extra setup

**Given** `requirements/parks.sample.json`
**When** it is copied into the app
**Then** its contents are byte-for-byte unchanged

**Given** the park data is loaded
**When** it is normalized
**Then** every park has safe defaults for missing, null, or empty fields per `park-data.md`, with no fabricated values
**And** parks are sorted A–Z and numbered 1–N (the number used on list cards and map markers)
**And** reading a missing nested field such as `location.address` never throws

**Given** the pixel art in `design/park-images/`
**When** the app is scaffolded
**Then** the images are moved to `src/assets/park-images/`
**And** each image URL is mapped at load time by filename stem to its bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`)
**And** a URL with no matching asset passes through unchanged

**Given** the app is running
**When** the URL is `/` or `/parks/:id`
**Then** the layout shell renders `<header>`, `<main>`, an `<aside aria-label="Parks">` region, and a map region labelled "Map of parks"
**And** `/parks/:id` identifies the selected park, an unknown id shows a "park not found" message with a way back to the list, and browser Back works

**Given** the park data fails to load
**When** the app renders
**Then** it shows "Loading parks…" while loading and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button on failure, never a blank screen

**Given** the design tokens and font in `design/README.md`
**When** Tailwind is configured
**Then** the colour tokens, the Public Sans font, and the amber focus-visible ring utility are available

### Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection

As a resident,
I want to browse every park in a list and open one without using the map,
So that I can find a park by mouse, touch, or keyboard alone.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the park data
**When** the list renders
**Then** all 12 parks appear A–Z inside a `<ul>`, including parks with missing fields
**And** the list has an "All parks" heading and a one-line hint

**Given** a park card
**When** it renders
**Then** it shows its number badge, name, address (or "Address not listed" in the muted colour), and the first three amenities as readable tags plus "+N more" when there are more

**Given** a keyboard user
**When** they press Tab through the list
**Then** each card is a single tab stop (one `<button>` or link) with a visible amber focus ring
**And** pressing Enter navigates to `/parks/:id`

**Given** a mouse or touch user
**When** they click or tap a card
**Then** the app navigates to that park's details

**Given** the user returns from details to the list
**When** the list renders again
**Then** focus moves to the card of the park they came from

### Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling

As a resident,
I want to see everything known about a park, and a clear note where information is missing,
So that I can decide whether to visit without being misled.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the user opens `/parks/:id`
**When** details render
**Then** an "All parks" back control appears first, then the photo area, the park name as the view's single `<h1>`, the address, an "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, fact tiles in a `<dl>` for Hours, Size (acres), and Rating ("4.7 / 5"), the About description, and all amenities as checked tags
**And** focus moves to the details heading when details open

**Given** a park with missing data
**When** details render
**Then** each case follows the `design/README.md` missing-data table:
- missing `location.address` (Highland Dog Park) → "Address not listed"
- `description: null` (Old Mill Botanical Garden) → "No description has been provided for this park."
- `rating: null` (Cedar Hill) → "Not yet rated", never 0 or empty stars
- `images: []` (Cedar Hill, East Ridge) → a dashed "No photos for this park yet" box
- missing `hours` or `acreage` → "Not listed"
- missing or empty `amenities` → the section is hidden or shows "No amenities listed"
- missing lat/lng → no OpenStreetMap link
**And** missing-value fact tiles use the muted style (missing background, dashed border, italic text)

**Given** an image fails to load
**When** the `<img>` fires `onError`
**Then** the no-photo box replaces it, with no broken-image icon

**Given** a park has images
**When** details render
**Then** the first image shows with a "Photo 1 of N" label and alt text naming the park

**Given** amenity tokens such as `dog-run`
**When** they render
**Then** they display as readable labels ("Dog run"), and unknown tokens are humanized the same way, never dropped

### Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection

As a resident,
I want to see where parks are on a map and open a park from its marker,
So that I can choose by location.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the app loads without location permission
**When** the map renders
**Then** it shows OpenStreetMap tiles via react-leaflet with visible attribution, fitted to the bounds of parks that have coordinates
**And** it never requests the user's location

**Given** parks with valid lat/lng
**When** the map renders
**Then** each has a marker showing its list number, focusable, with the accessible name "N. Park name"
**And** a park with no valid coordinates has no marker but stays in the list
**And** markers render correctly under Vite (no broken default icon)

**Given** desktop width (≥ 768px)
**When** the user activates a marker by click or keyboard
**Then** the app navigates to `/parks/:id` and the sidebar shows that park's details

**Given** a park is selected from either the list or the map
**When** the map renders
**Then** that park's marker is amber and larger than the others

**Given** phone width (< 768px)
**When** the user taps a marker on the Map tab
**Then** a preview card shows "Park N of 12", the name, the address, the hours, a "View park details" button, and a close button labelled "Close park preview"

**Given** map controls
**When** they render
**Then** zoom and close buttons have `aria-label`s and touch targets of at least 44×44px

### Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied

As a resident on a phone or using a keyboard or screen reader,
I want the app to match the designed layouts and work well in every mode,
So that the main tasks are easy everywhere.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the screens in `design/screens/`
**When** the app is compared against them at 1280×800 and 390×844
**Then** the layout, tokens, type sizes, corner radius, and spacing match `design/README.md`, except where the SPEC overrides the design

**Given** desktop width (≥ 768px)
**When** the app renders
**Then** a green header shows the app name, subtitle, and park count, with a ~400px independently scrolling sidebar and the map filling the rest

**Given** phone width (< 768px)
**When** the app renders
**Then** a single column shows a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`, List by default
**And** details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link in the header

**Given** widths of 375px and 1280px
**When** the user completes list → details → back
**Then** there is no horizontal scroll and every interactive element shows the amber focus-visible ring

**Given** the whole app
**When** it is checked for accessibility
**Then** there are no clickable `<div>`s, there is one `<h1>` per view, every icon-only button has an `aria-label`, touch targets are at least 44×44px, and text contrast is at least 4.5:1

### Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

As the interviewer,
I want evidence the app was checked and a README I can run and judge from,
So that I can evaluate usefulness, decisions, and verification.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Vitest + React Testing Library
**When** `npm test` runs
**Then** tests pass that cover every known gap in `park-data.md`: Old Mill's null description, Cedar Hill's empty images and null rating, East Ridge's empty images, Highland's missing address, an image that fails to load, and an unmatched image URL
**And** a test covers the keyboard flow: Tab to a list card, Enter, the details heading receives focus, and "All parks" returns focus to the originating card
**And** a test covers the image URL transformation without mutating the source data

**Given** the README
**When** the interviewer reads it
**Then** it contains run instructions, what works, what was left out, decisions (stack, routing, map tiles), assumptions from SPEC.md, data changes (the image URL transformation; the JSON file is unchanged), known issues (including Cedar Hill's placeholder-looking coordinates and the OSM tile usage policy), how the result was checked, approximate time spent, and next steps before public use
**And** it says where the unredacted Claude Code and Claude Design transcripts and the design files live in the repo

# Find a Park: design handoff

UI designs for the Find a Park take-home. They were drawn on a Claude design canvas before any code was written, and they are the visual spec for the React + Tailwind + Leaflet build. The full AI transcript of the design session is in the repo alongside this folder.

**These are reference designs, not code.** Build with React, Tailwind and react-leaflet (no component library), and use the screenshots plus the rules below. Where the brief and these designs disagree, the brief wins.

## Contents

| File | What it shows |
|---|---|
| `screens/01-desktop-list-map.png` | Desktop default: park list on the left, map on the right |
| `screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png` | Desktop with a park selected: details replace the list, the marker is highlighted |
| `screens/03-desktop-missing-data.png` | Desktop details for a park with missing data (Cedar Hill: no photos, no rating) |
| `screens/04-phone-list.png` | Phone, List tab |
| `screens/05-phone-map-selected.png` | Phone, Map tab, with a park selected (preview card) |
| `screens/06-phone-details.png` | Phone details, full data (Prospect Park) |
| `screens/07-phone-missing-data.png` | Phone details, missing data (Cedar Hill) |
| `source/*.dc.html` | Canvas source files, for reference only. They need the canvas runtime and are **not** meant to run in the app. |

Screenshots are at 2x: desktop at 1280×800, phone at 390×844. The map in every screenshot is a drawn stand-in. The real app uses Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles.

## Layout and behaviour

### Desktop (≥ 768px, Tailwind `md:`)

- A green header with the app name, a subtitle and the park count.
- Two columns:
  - **Left sidebar (~400px):** scrolls on its own.
  - **Map:** fills the rest of the screen.
- **Sidebar, list mode:** an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, then one card per park.
- **Selecting a park** from either the list or the map:
  - The sidebar switches to details mode, starting with an "All parks" back button.
  - That park's marker turns amber and gets bigger.
  - The URL should change too (e.g. `?park=prospect-park` or `/parks/:id`), so details can be linked and the browser Back button works.
- **Going back to the list:** move focus to the card of the park you came from.
- **Opening details:** move focus to the details heading, so keyboard and screen-reader users land in the right place.

### Phone (< 768px)

- One column. A **List / Map** toggle sits under the header, made of two real `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`. List is the default.
- **Tapping a marker** opens a preview card at the bottom of the map. It shows the park's position in the list ("Park 8 of 12"), its name, address and hours, a **View park details** button and a close button.
- **Details** take the whole screen, with an "All parks" back link in the header.

### List ↔ map link

- Parks are sorted A–Z and numbered 1–12. The same number appears on the list card and on the map marker, so the two views always match.
- Every marker is focusable and named "8. Prospect Park" for screen readers. Use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside it, or set `keyboard: true` and `alt`/`title` on the markers.
- The list is the main way to choose a park. The map is never the only way.

## Park card (list)

- Number badge, park name (17px bold), address, and the first three amenities as tags. If there are more, add a "+N more" tag.
- The whole card is a single `<button>` (or a link once there is routing), so it gets keyboard focus and one Tab stop.

## Details

The details view shows, from top to bottom:

1. Photo area (see the missing-data rules below)
2. Park name as the page heading (26–28px, 800 weight), then the address
3. An **Open in OpenStreetMap** link built from the park's lat/lng (`https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`). This was added during design and is easy to drop if you don't want it.
4. Three fact tiles: **Hours**, **Size** (acres), **Rating** ("4.7 / 5")
5. **About** (the description)
6. **Amenities**: all of them, as tags with a check mark. Turn the raw values into labels by replacing `-` with a space and capitalising the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run").

## Missing data: never invent a value

The brief requires this, and the sample data has every one of these cases.

| Field | When missing / null / empty | Show |
|---|---|---|
| `location.address` | Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" in the secondary text colour |
| `description` | Old Mill Botanical Garden (`null`) | "No description has been provided for this park." |
| `rating` | Cedar Hill (`null`) | "Not yet rated" (do not show 0 or blank stars) |
| `images` | Cedar Hill, East Ridge (`[]`) | A dashed box: image icon + "No photos for this park yet" |
| `images` failing to load | **Every** sample URL (`images.example.com` doesn't exist) | The same fallback, triggered by `onError` on `<img>`; never a broken-image icon |
| `hours` | not in the sample, but handle it | "Not listed" |
| `acreage` | not in the sample, but handle it | "Not listed" |
| `amenities` | not in the sample, but handle it | Hide the Amenities section, or show "No amenities listed" |
| `location.lat/lng` | not in the sample, but handle it | Keep the park in the list, leave it off the map, and hide the map link |

Fact tiles with a missing value use the muted look from screen 07: `#F0EDE5` background, dashed `#C9C4B6` border, italic text. *Remark: the desktop screenshot (03) shows "Not yet rated" with the normal tile style. That is a mistake in the mock, and the phone version is the intended look.*

Wrap every field in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`) so the rules stay in one place. Never let a missing nested field crash the app (`park.location?.address`).

## Design tokens (Tailwind)

Add these under `theme.extend` (or as CSS variables with Tailwind v4 `@theme`):

```js
colors: {
  paper:   '#F6F4EE', // page background
  surface: '#FFFFFF', // cards, tiles
  line:    '#DAD6CB', // card borders
  ink:     '#1B2620', // main text
  muted:   '#4F5B54', // secondary text (≈7:1 on white)
  forest:  { DEFAULT: '#1E5B3F', dark: '#123826' }, // header, primary buttons, markers, links
  tag:     { bg: '#E9EFE7', fg: '#23472F' },         // amenity tags
  amber:   '#B45309', // selected marker and focus ring
  missing: { bg: '#F0EDE5', line: '#C9C4B6' },       // missing-value tiles, no-photo box
  map:     { land: '#E4E8DF', water: '#B9D3DC' },    // only for the drawn map; real tiles replace these
},
fontFamily: { sans: ['"Public Sans"', '"Segoe UI"', 'system-ui', 'sans-serif'] },
```

- **Font:** Public Sans from Google Fonts, weights 400/500/600/700/800.
- **Type sizes:**
  - Body 15–16px; card titles 17px bold
  - Details heading 26–28px (800 weight); section headings 17–18px
  - Small labels 13px; nothing smaller except the map attribution
- **Corner radius:** cards 10px, buttons 8–10px, tags fully rounded.
- **Spacing:** sidebar padding 20px (16px on phone), 10px between cards, 14–16px inside cards.

## Accessibility

These checks match what the brief evaluates:

- **Focus ring:** every interactive element gets a visible ring, `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`. Never remove outlines without putting this ring in their place.
- **Touch targets:** at least 44×44px, including the zoom buttons, the close button and the back button.
- **Real elements:** use `<button>`, `<a href>`, `<ul>/<li>` for the list, `<dl>/<dt>/<dd>` for the fact tiles, and one `<h1>` per view. No clickable `<div>`s.
- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, an `<aside aria-label="Parks">` for the sidebar, and a map section labelled "Map of parks".
- **Labels:** icon-only buttons need an `aria-label` ("Zoom in", "Close park preview").
- **Contrast:** text has at least 4.5:1 contrast. The colours above meet that, including white on forest.
- **Location:** never ask for the user's location; the app must work without it. Centre the map on a fixed view, or fit it to the parks' bounds.

## Remarks and open decisions

- **Municipality:** the parks span Brooklyn, Manhattan, Queens and the Bronx and are mostly fictional (Prospect Park is real). The designs call the area "New York City area". Say so in the project README.
- **Cedar Hill coordinates** (40.7128, -74.006) are exactly the standard "New York City" point, so they are probably a placeholder. The park is shown as given; mention it in Known issues.
- **Short addresses** such as "Ridge Rd" and "Shore Pkwy" are shown as given, without adding a city.
- **Not designed:**
  - Search and filters (optional in the brief)
  - Current-location support (optional)
  - A loading state and an error state for when `parks.json` fails to load. Both are worth adding: a short "Loading parks…" message and a "Couldn't load parks. Try again." message with a retry button, using the same card style.
- **Hours** are shown exactly as written in the data ("Dawn to dusk", "24 hours"). An "Open now" badge was left out on purpose, because parsing these strings reliably is out of scope.
- **Photos:** there is no gallery. The mock shows the first photo with a "Photo 1 of N" label. Showing a single image, or a simple row of thumbnails, is fine.
- **Map tiles:** OpenStreetMap's tile server needs no API key; keep its attribution visible. For real public use, switch to a tile provider that allows production traffic. That belongs in the README's next steps.

1	# Generate Tracking
2	
3	Discovery is your call; everything after it is the script's.
4	
5	1. Identify the epic files. The gate inventory already surfaced them — typically `epics.md`, `epic-*.md`, or a sharded `epics/` folder in `{planning_artifacts}`, but trust content over filename. If both a whole document and a sharded version exist, ask which is current rather than guessing.
6	2. Run the script, passing every epic file:
7	
8	   ```
9	   uv run {skill-root}/scripts/sprint_plan.py generate \
10	     --epic-file <path> [--epic-file <path> ...] \
11	     --status-file {implementation_artifacts}/sprint-status.yaml \
12	     --stories-dir {implementation_artifacts} \
13	     --project "{project_name}" --date "{date}"
14	   ```
15	
16	   `{date}` must be `MM-DD-YYYY HH:MM` — the format the staleness check parses. The script owns parsing (`## Epic N:` / `### Story N.M: Title` → kebab-case keys; fenced code blocks ignored), ordering (epic, its stories, its retrospective), merging with any existing file (preserve advanced statuses, never downgrade; legacy v6 values like `drafted`/`contexted` are normalized to their modern meaning, never reset; `action_items`, custom keys, and user comments carried through; `project_key`/`tracking_system`/`story_location` kept from the existing file unless overridden by flag), story-file detection (a story file on disk floors its status at `ready-for-dev`), atomic writes, and post-write validation. It prints a JSON report. Add `--dry-run` to preview — the report's `in_sync`, `new_entries`, `dropped_orphans`, `illegal`, and `legacy_mapped` fields answer "is tracking in sync?" without writing.
17	
18	3. Read the JSON report and act on it — this is where judgment re-enters:
19	   - `warnings` about unparsed Epic/Story-like headings mean the epic file deviates from the standard format. Show the user, fix the headings together (or accept the omission), and rerun.
20	   - `dropped_orphans` are entries that existed in the old status file but match nothing in the epics — usually renames. Each carries its old status; reconcile with the user, then transplant by rerunning with `--set <new-key>=<old-status>`.
21	   - If the epics defeat the parser entirely (a format the regexes can't see), fall back to building the file yourself against `sprint-status-template.yaml`, and tell the user the deterministic path didn't apply.
22	
23	## Report
24	
25	Present the result from the script's JSON in `{communication_language}`: file path, epic/story counts, status breakdown, anything upgraded from disk. Suggest next steps — review the file, `bmad-build` to start the first story, rerun this skill anytime to refresh after epics change.
26

# STATUS DEFINITIONS:
# ==================
# Epic Status:
#   - backlog: Epic not yet started
#   - in-progress: Epic actively being worked on
#   - done: All stories in epic completed

# Story Status:
#   - backlog: Story only exists in epic file
#   - ready-for-dev: Story file created, ready for development
#   - in-progress: Developer actively working on implementation
#   - review: Implementation complete, ready for review
#   - done: Story completed

# Retrospective Status:
#   - optional: Can be completed but not required
#   - done: Retrospective has been completed

# Action Item Status:
#   - open: Committed during a retrospective, not yet addressed
#   - in-progress: Actively being worked on
#   - done: Completed

# WORKFLOW NOTES:
# ===============
# - Epic transitions to 'in-progress' automatically when its first story starts (via build's sprint sync)

1	# Build New Preview Workflow
2	
3	**Goal:** Turn user intent into a hardened, reviewable artifact.
4	
5	**CRITICAL:** If a step directs you to another snapshot file, read it fully and follow it. No exceptions.
6	
7	Subagents, when the capability is available, are an important part of this workflow. Use them as directed by the workflow steps.
8	If you need an explicit user instruction to run them, ask once now for the whole workflow run.
9	
10	## READY FOR DEVELOPMENT STANDARD
11	
12	A specification is "Ready for Development" when:
13	
14	- **Actionable**: Every task has a file path and specific action.
15	- **Logical**: Tasks ordered by dependency.
16	- **Testable**: All ACs use Given/When/Then.
17	- **Complete**: No placeholders or TBDs.
18	- **Sufficient**: No known requirement, acceptance, dependency, or implementation gaps remain unresolved.
19	- **Coherent**: No unresolved ambiguities or internal contradictions.
20	
21	## SCOPE STANDARD
22	
23	A specification should target a **single user-facing goal** within **900–1600 tokens**:
24	
25	- **Single goal**: One cohesive feature, even if it spans multiple layers/files. Multi-goal means >=2 **top-level independent shippable deliverables** — each could be reviewed, tested, and merged as a separate PR without breaking the others. Never count surface verbs, "and" conjunctions, or noun phrases. Never split cross-layer implementation details inside one user goal.
26	  - Split: "add dark mode toggle AND refactor auth to JWT AND build admin dashboard"
27	  - Don't split: "add validation and display errors" / "support drag-and-drop AND paste AND retry"
28	- **900–1600 tokens**: Optimal range for LLM consumption. Below 900 risks ambiguity; above 1600 risks context-rot in implementation agents.
29	- **Neither limit is a gate.** Both are proposals with user override.
30	
31	## Conventions
32	
33	- Every operational cross-file reference in this workflow is an absolute snapshot path. Open it directly; do not resolve it relative to a skill directory.
34	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
35	- Whenever this workflow captures or records a version-control revision, obtain the full canonical identifier directly from version control and preserve it verbatim.
36	
37	## On Activation
38	
39	### Step 1: Execute Prepend Steps
40	
41	Execute each of these steps in order before proceeding (`_None._` means skip):
42	
43	_None._
44	
45	### Step 2: Load Persistent Facts
46	
47	Treat every entry below as foundational context you carry for the rest of the workflow run. Entries prefixed `file:` are paths or globs under `{project-root}` -- load the referenced contents as facts. All other entries are facts verbatim (`_None._` means none):
48	
49	_None._
50	
51	### Step 3: Execute Append Steps
52	
53	Execute each of these steps in order (`_None._` means skip):
54	
55	_None._
56	
57	## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
58	
59	This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution:
60	
61	- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is self-contained and followed exactly
62	- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only load the current step file
63	- **Sequential Enforcement**: Complete steps in order, no skipping
64	- **State Tracking**: Persist progress via spec frontmatter and in-memory variables
65	- **Append-Only Building**: Build artifacts incrementally
66	
67	### Step Processing Rules
68	
69	1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Read the entire step file before acting
70	2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute sections in order
71	3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: Halt at checkpoints and wait for human
72	4. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
73	
74	### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
75	
76	- **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
77	- **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
78	- **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
79	- **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
80	- **ALWAYS** halt at checkpoints and wait for human input

[tool result for toolu_01MRQndCvT3mbmMCGBmRTy2j]
1	# Compile Epic Context
2	
3	**Task**
4	Given an epic number, the epics file, the planning artifacts directory, and a desired output path, compile a clean, focused, developer-ready context file (`epic-<N>-context.md`).
5	
6	**Steps**
7	
8	1. Read the epics file and extract the target epic's title, goal, and list of stories.
9	2. Scan the planning artifacts directory for the standard files (PRD, architecture, UX/design, product brief).
10	3. Pull only the information relevant to this epic.
11	4. Write the compiled context to the exact output path using the format below.
12	
13	## Exact Output Format
14	
15	Use these headings:
16	
17	```markdown
18	# Epic {N} Context: {Epic Title}
19	
20	<!-- Compiled from planning artifacts. Edit freely. Regenerate with compile-epic-context if planning docs change. -->
21	
22	## Goal
23	
24	{One clear paragraph: what this epic achieves and why it matters.}
25	
26	## Stories
27	
28	- Story X.Y: Brief title only
29	- ...
30	
31	## Requirements & Constraints
32	
33	{Relevant functional/non-functional requirements and success criteria for this epic (describe by purpose, not source).}
34	
35	## Technical Decisions
36	
37	{Key architecture decisions, constraints, patterns, data models, and conventions relevant to this epic.}
38	
39	## UX & Interaction Patterns
40	
41	{Relevant UX flows, interaction patterns, and design constraints (omit section entirely if nothing relevant).}
42	
43	## Cross-Story Dependencies
44	
45	{Dependencies between stories in this epic or with other epics/systems (omit if none).}
46	```
47	
48	## Rules
49	
50	- **Scope aggressively.** Include only what a developer working on any story in this epic actually needs. When in doubt, leave it out — the developer can always read the full planning doc.
51	- **Describe by purpose, not by source.** Write "API responses must include pagination metadata" not "Per PRD section 3.2.1, pagination is required." Planning doc internals will change; the constraint won't.
52	- **No full copies.** Never quote source documents, section numbers, or paste large blocks verbatim. Always distill.
53	- **No story-level details.** The story list is for orientation only. Individual story specs handle the details.
54	- **Nothing derivable from the codebase.** Don't document what a developer can learn by reading the code.
55	- **Be concise and actionable.** Target 800–1500 tokens total. This file loads into build's context alongside other material.
56	- **Never hallucinate content.** If source material doesn't say something, don't invent it.
57	- **Omit empty sections entirely**, except Goal and Stories, which are always required.
58	
59	## Error handling
60	
61	- **If the epics file is missing or the target epic is not found:** write nothing and report the problem to the calling agent. Goal and Stories cannot be populated without a usable epics file.
62	- **If planning artifacts are missing or empty:** still produce the file with Goal and Stories populated from the epics file, and note the gap in the Goal section. Never hallucinate content to fill missing sections.
63

# Find a Park: design handoff

UI designs for the Find a Park take-home. They were drawn on a Claude design canvas before any code was written, and they are the visual spec for the React + Tailwind + Leaflet build. The full AI transcript of the design session is in the repo alongside this folder.

**These are reference designs, not code.** Build with React, Tailwind and react-leaflet (no component library), and use the screenshots plus the rules below. Where the brief and these designs disagree, the brief wins.

## Contents

| File | What it shows |
|---|---|
| `screens/01-desktop-list-map.png` | Desktop default: park list on the left, map on the right |
| `screens/02-desktop-park-selected.png` | Desktop with a park selected: details replace the list, the marker is highlighted |
| `screens/03-desktop-missing-data.png` | Desktop details for a park with missing data (Cedar Hill: no photos, no rating) |
| `screens/04-phone-list.png` | Phone, List tab |
| `screens/05-phone-map-selected.png` | Phone, Map tab, with a park selected (preview card) |
| `screens/06-phone-details.png` | Phone details, full data (Prospect Park) |
| `screens/07-phone-missing-data.png` | Phone details, missing data (Cedar Hill) |
| `source/*.dc.html` | Canvas source files, for reference only. They need the canvas runtime and are **not** meant to run in the app. |

Screenshots are at 2x: desktop at 1280×800, phone at 390×844. The map in every screenshot is a drawn stand-in. The real app uses Leaflet with OpenStreetMap tiles.

## Layout and behaviour

### Desktop (≥ 768px, Tailwind `md:`)

- A green header with the app name, a subtitle and the park count.
- Two columns:
  - **Left sidebar (~400px):** scrolls on its own.
  - **Map:** fills the rest of the screen.
- **Sidebar, list mode:** an "All parks" heading, a one-line hint, then one card per park.
- **Selecting a park** from either the list or the map:
  - The sidebar switches to details mode, starting with an "All parks" back button.
  - That park's marker turns amber and gets bigger.
  - The URL should change too (e.g. `?park=prospect-park` or `/parks/:id`), so details can be linked and the browser Back button works.
- **Going back to the list:** move focus to the card of the park you came from.
- **Opening details:** move focus to the details heading, so keyboard and screen-reader users land in the right place.

### Phone (< 768px)

- One column. A **List / Map** toggle sits under the header, made of two real `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`. List is the default.
- **Tapping a marker** opens a preview card at the bottom of the map. It shows the park's position in the list ("Park 8 of 12"), its name, address and hours, a **View park details** button and a close button.
- **Details** take the whole screen, with an "All parks" back link in the header.

### List ↔ map link

- Parks are sorted A–Z and numbered 1–12. The same number appears on the list card and on the map marker, so the two views always match.
- Every marker is focusable and named "8. Prospect Park" for screen readers. Use `L.divIcon` with a `<button>` inside it, or set `keyboard: true` and `alt`/`title` on the markers.
- The list is the main way to choose a park. The map is never the only way.

## Park card (list)

- Number badge, park name (17px bold), address, and the first three amenities as tags. If there are more, add a "+N more" tag.
- The whole card is a single `<button>` (or a link once there is routing), so it gets keyboard focus and one Tab stop.

## Details

The details view shows, from top to bottom:

1. Photo area (see the missing-data rules below)
2. Park name as the page heading (26–28px, 800 weight), then the address
3. An **Open in OpenStreetMap** link built from the park's lat/lng (`https://www.openstreetmap.org/?mlat={lat}&mlon={lng}#map=16/{lat}/{lng}`). This was added during design and is easy to drop if you don't want it.
4. Three fact tiles: **Hours**, **Size** (acres), **Rating** ("4.7 / 5")
5. **About** (the description)
6. **Amenities**: all of them, as tags with a check mark. Turn the raw values into labels by replacing `-` with a space and capitalising the first letter (`dog-run` → "Dog run").

## Missing data: never invent a value

The brief requires this, and the sample data has every one of these cases.

| Field | When missing / null / empty | Show |
|---|---|---|
| `location.address` | Highland Dog Park | "Address not listed" in the secondary text colour |
| `description` | Old Mill Botanical Garden (`null`) | "No description has been provided for this park." |
| `rating` | Cedar Hill (`null`) | "Not yet rated" (do not show 0 or blank stars) |
| `images` | Cedar Hill, East Ridge (`[]`) | A dashed box: image icon + "No photos for this park yet" |
| `images` failing to load | **Every** sample URL (`images.example.com` doesn't exist) | The same fallback, triggered by `onError` on `<img>`; never a broken-image icon |
| `hours` | not in the sample, but handle it | "Not listed" |
| `acreage` | not in the sample, but handle it | "Not listed" |
| `amenities` | not in the sample, but handle it | Hide the Amenities section, or show "No amenities listed" |
| `location.lat/lng` | not in the sample, but handle it | Keep the park in the list, leave it off the map, and hide the map link |

Fact tiles with a missing value use the muted look from screen 07: `#F0EDE5` background, dashed `#C9C4B6` border, italic text. *Remark: the desktop screenshot (03) shows "Not yet rated" with the normal tile style. That is a mistake in the mock, and the phone version is the intended look.*

Wrap every field in a small helper (e.g. `display(value, fallback)`) so the rules stay in one place. Never let a missing nested field crash the app (`park.location?.address`).

## Design tokens (Tailwind)

Add these under `theme.extend` (or as CSS variables with Tailwind v4 `@theme`):

```js
colors: {
  paper:   '#F6F4EE', // page background
  surface: '#FFFFFF', // cards, tiles
  line:    '#DAD6CB', // card borders
  ink:     '#1B2620', // main text
  muted:   '#4F5B54', // secondary text (≈7:1 on white)
  forest:  { DEFAULT: '#1E5B3F', dark: '#123826' }, // header, primary buttons, markers, links
  tag:     { bg: '#E9EFE7', fg: '#23472F' },         // amenity tags
  amber:   '#B45309', // selected marker and focus ring
  missing: { bg: '#F0EDE5', line: '#C9C4B6' },       // missing-value tiles, no-photo box
  map:     { land: '#E4E8DF', water: '#B9D3DC' },    // only for the drawn map; real tiles replace these
},
fontFamily: { sans: ['"Public Sans"', '"Segoe UI"', 'system-ui', 'sans-serif'] },
```

- **Font:** Public Sans from Google Fonts, weights 400/500/600/700/800.
- **Type sizes:**
  - Body 15–16px; card titles 17px bold
  - Details heading 26–28px (800 weight); section headings 17–18px
  - Small labels 13px; nothing smaller except the map attribution
- **Corner radius:** cards 10px, buttons 8–10px, tags fully rounded.
- **Spacing:** sidebar padding 20px (16px on phone), 10px between cards, 14–16px inside cards.

## Accessibility

These checks match what the brief evaluates:

- **Focus ring:** every interactive element gets a visible ring, `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-[3px] focus-visible:outline-amber focus-visible:outline-offset-2`. Never remove outlines without putting this ring in their place.
- **Touch targets:** at least 44×44px, including the zoom buttons, the close button and the back button.
- **Real elements:** use `<button>`, `<a href>`, `<ul>/<li>` for the list, `<dl>/<dt>/<dd>` for the fact tiles, and one `<h1>` per view. No clickable `<div>`s.
- **Landmarks:** `<header>`, `<main>`, an `<aside aria-label="Parks">` for the sidebar, and a map section labelled "Map of parks".
- **Labels:** icon-only buttons need an `aria-label` ("Zoom in", "Close park preview").
- **Contrast:** text has at least 4.5:1 contrast. The colours above meet that, including white on forest.
- **Location:** never ask for the user's location; the app must work without it. Centre the map on a fixed view, or fit it to the parks' bounds.

## Remarks and open decisions

- **Municipality:** the parks span Brooklyn, Manhattan, Queens and the Bronx and are mostly fictional (Prospect Park is real). The designs call the area "New York City area". Say so in the project README.
- **Cedar Hill coordinates** (40.7128, -74.006) are exactly the standard "New York City" point, so they are probably a placeholder. The park is shown as given; mention it in Known issues.
- **Short addresses** such as "Ridge Rd" and "Shore Pkwy" are shown as given, without adding a city.
- **Not designed:**
  - Search and filters (optional in the brief)
  - Current-location support (optional)
  - A loading state and an error state for when `parks.json` fails to load. Both are worth adding: a short "Loading parks…" message and a "Couldn't load parks. Try again." message with a retry button, using the same card style.
- **Hours** are shown exactly as written in the data ("Dawn to dusk", "24 hours"). An "Open now" badge was left out on purpose, because parsing these strings reliably is out of scope.
- **Photos:** there is no gallery. The mock shows the first photo with a "Photo 1 of N" label. Showing a single image, or a simple row of thumbnails, is fine.
- **Map tiles:** OpenStreetMap's tile server needs no API key; keep its attribution visible. For real public use, switch to a tile provider that allows production traffic. That belongs in the README's next steps.

> **Canonical contract.** This SPEC and the files in `companions:` are the complete, preservation-validated contract for what to build, test, and validate. Source documents listed in frontmatter are for traceability — consult them only if you need narrative rationale or prose color this contract intentionally omits.

# Find a Park

## Why

A mandate to meet: a 2-hour take-home brief (Granicus Lighthouse) to build a small web app that helps residents find local parks. Evaluation weighs usefulness and accessibility for residents, clear communication of decisions, maintainable code that handles missing data, accountable AI use, and evidence of review and verification. A modest app that works and is well explained beats an ambitious one with broken core features.

## Capabilities

- **CAP-1**
  - **intent:** A resident sees where parks are on a map and can get from a marker to that park's details.
  - **success:** Every park with valid lat/lng has a marker; activating a marker opens the park's details or a clear link to them.
- **CAP-2**
  - **intent:** A resident browses all parks in a list and selects one without using the map.
  - **success:** All parks in the data appear, including ones with missing fields; a list item opens details by mouse, touch, and keyboard (Tab to it, Enter to open).
- **CAP-3**
  - **intent:** A resident views the available information for a park: name, address, description, amenities, hours, images, acreage, rating.
  - **success:** Each of the 12 sample parks renders; null, missing, or empty fields are omitted or labelled as not listed, never invented; broken image URLs degrade without a broken-image icon or crash.
- **CAP-4**
  - **intent:** The main tasks (find a park, open its details) work on phone and desktop, by keyboard, and with assistive technology.
  - **success:** At 375px width and at desktop width the list→details flow works with no horizontal scroll; every interactive element shows a visible focus indicator; regions and controls have readable text and clear labels.
- **CAP-5**
  - **intent:** The interviewer can run, understand, and judge the submission from its README.
  - **success:** README contains run instructions, what works, what was left out, decisions, assumptions, data/schema changes, known issues, how the result was checked, approximate time spent, and next steps before public use.

## Constraints

- Hard 2-hour box for the whole submission, including testing and README; stop at 2 hours and document what is unfinished.
- Must work without the resident's location.
- No private credentials in the repo; services must be keyless or document how the interviewer supplies a key.
- Data fields may be missing or null and arrays empty; the UI must not crash or fabricate details.
- The list and details view must be a complete alternative to map markers.
- Use the supplied `parks.sample.json`; document any extension of data or schema in the README.
- Unredacted AI conversation logs from every tool used are part of the submission: the developer manually exports the Claude Code transcript before each fresh story context, and exports the Claude Design transcript; the generated designs are committed in `design/`.
- Visual design follows `design/README.md` and `design/screens/`; where they conflict with this SPEC, this SPEC wins.

## Non-goals

- Backend, accounts, hosted deployment.
- Search, filters, current-location support — only if the core is done with time remaining.
- Editing or contributing park data.

## Success signal

- On a phone-width viewport and on desktop, a resident can open any of the 12 sample parks from the list using only the keyboard, and from a map marker by click/tap, and see its details with missing fields handled cleanly; the interviewer runs it from the README with one install and one start command.

## Assumptions

- Municipality is the NYC-area set implied by the sample coordinates; the app is titled generically ("Find a Park").
- Parks without valid lat/lng still appear in the list and details, just without a marker.
- A data-load failure shows an error message, not a blank screen.
# Stack

All choices confirmed by the user.

| Concern | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| UI | React | User decision. |
| Build tool | Vite | Fast scaffold; one `npm install` + `npm run dev`. |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS, mobile-first | User decision; responsive by default. |
| Map | `react-leaflet` + Leaflet | User decision; no API key. |
| Tiles | OpenStreetMap standard tiles, with attribution | Keyless; satisfies the no-credentials constraint. |
| Data | Static import of `parks.sample.json` (copied into the app) | No backend. |
| Routing | Path-based `/parks/:id` for park details; `/` is the list | Linkable details, working Back. Supersedes the `?park=` option in `design/README.md`. |
| Tests | Vitest + React Testing Library | Cover every known gap in `park-data.md` (null description, empty images, null rating, missing address, failing image URLs) plus the keyboard list→details flow. |

## Conventions

- Map initial view fits the bounds of parks that have coordinates; never depends on geolocation.
- Leaflet's default marker icons need explicit asset imports under Vite, or markers render as broken images.
- Visual design, tokens, missing-data display copy, and accessibility rules come from `design/README.md` (adopted companion) and `design/screens/`.
- Park images: pixel art lives in `src/assets/park-images/` (moved from `design/park-images/`) and is resolved via the load-time URL transformation in `park-data.md`; the image-failure fallback is still required.
# Park data

Source: `requirements/parks.sample.json` — an array of 12 park objects.

## Shape

| Field | Type | May be missing/null/empty |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string (slug) | treat as required |
| `name` | string | treat as required |
| `description` | string | yes |
| `location.lat`, `location.lng` | number | yes (no marker if absent) |
| `location.address` | string | yes |
| `amenities` | string[] (kebab-case tokens, e.g. `dog-run`) | yes / empty |
| `hours` | free-text string (e.g. `Dawn to dusk`, `24 hours`) | yes |
| `images` | string[] URLs | yes / empty |
| `acreage` | number | yes |
| `rating` | number (0–5) | yes |

Amenity tokens display as readable labels (`dog-run` → "Dog run"); unknown tokens fall back to the token humanized, never dropped.

## Image URL transformation

`parks.sample.json` is never edited. At load time each image URL is mapped by filename stem to a bundled asset: `https://images.example.com/prospect-1.jpg` → `src/assets/park-images/prospect-1.png` (source art: `design/park-images/`). A URL with no matching asset passes through unchanged, so the `onError` fallback still applies. All 12 sample URLs have a match. Describe this transformation in the README.

## Known gaps in the sample (test fixtures)

| Park | Gap |
|---|---|
| `old-mill-botanical-garden` | `description: null` |
| `cedar-hill-nature-preserve` | `images: []`, `rating: null` |
| `east-ridge-trailhead` | `images: []` |
| `highland-dog-park` | `location.address` missing |
| all | image URLs are `images.example.com` placeholders that do not resolve; the transformation above maps them to bundled art, and an unmapped URL must still fall back cleanly |

# Find a Park: Epics and Stories

Derived from `SPEC-find-local-parks` and its companions. Every story must also be read with:

- `../specs/spec-find-local-parks/SPEC.md`: capabilities CAP-1 to CAP-5, constraints, non-goals
- `../specs/spec-find-local-parks/stack.md`: stack and conventions
- `../specs/spec-find-local-parks/park-data.md`: data shape, the image URL transformation, known gaps
- `../../design/README.md` and `../../design/screens/`: layout, tokens, missing-data wording, accessibility rules

Where the design and the SPEC disagree, the SPEC wins.

## Requirements Coverage

| Capability | Stories |
|---|---|
| CAP-1 Map | 1.4 |
| CAP-2 List | 1.2 |
| CAP-3 Details and missing data | 1.1 (data layer), 1.3 |
| CAP-4 Accessible, responsive UI | 1.2, 1.3, 1.4 (baseline), 1.5 (full pass) |
| CAP-5 README | 1.6 |

Cross-cutting constraints that apply to every story: no geolocation, no credentials, never invent missing data, `parks.sample.json` is never edited, keyboard focus is always visible.

## Epic 1: Find a Park

A resident can find a local park on a map or in a list and open its details on a phone or desktop, by mouse, touch, or keyboard, with missing data handled honestly. The interviewer can run and judge the result from the README.

### Story 1.1: Scaffold app and park data layer

As a developer,
I want a running Vite + React + Tailwind app with a safe park data layer, routing, and bundled images,
So that every later story builds on data that cannot crash the UI.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** a fresh clone
**When** the developer runs one install command and one start command
**Then** the app starts locally with no API keys or extra setup

**Given** `requirements/parks.sample.json`
**When** it is copied into the app
**Then** its contents are byte-for-byte unchanged

**Given** the park data is loaded
**When** it is normalized
**Then** every park has safe defaults for missing, null, or empty fields per `park-data.md`, with no fabricated values
**And** parks are sorted A–Z and numbered 1–N (the number used on list cards and map markers)
**And** reading a missing nested field such as `location.address` never throws

**Given** the pixel art in `design/park-images/`
**When** the app is scaffolded
**Then** the images are moved to `src/assets/park-images/`
**And** each image URL is mapped at load time by filename stem to its bundled asset (`.../prospect-1.jpg` → `park-images/prospect-1.png`)
**And** a URL with no matching asset passes through unchanged

**Given** the app is running
**When** the URL is `/` or `/parks/:id`
**Then** the layout shell renders `<header>`, `<main>`, an `<aside aria-label="Parks">` region, and a map region labelled "Map of parks"
**And** `/parks/:id` identifies the selected park, an unknown id shows a "park not found" message with a way back to the list, and browser Back works

**Given** the park data fails to load
**When** the app renders
**Then** it shows "Loading parks…" while loading and "Couldn't load parks. Try again." with a retry button on failure, never a blank screen

**Given** the design tokens and font in `design/README.md`
**When** Tailwind is configured
**Then** the colour tokens, the Public Sans font, and the amber focus-visible ring utility are available

### Story 1.2: Park list with keyboard selection

As a resident,
I want to browse every park in a list and open one without using the map,
So that I can find a park by mouse, touch, or keyboard alone.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the park data
**When** the list renders
**Then** all 12 parks appear A–Z inside a `<ul>`, including parks with missing fields
**And** the list has an "All parks" heading and a one-line hint

**Given** a park card
**When** it renders
**Then** it shows its number badge, name, address (or "Address not listed" in the muted colour), and the first three amenities as readable tags plus "+N more" when there are more

**Given** a keyboard user
**When** they press Tab through the list
**Then** each card is a single tab stop (one `<button>` or link) with a visible amber focus ring
**And** pressing Enter navigates to `/parks/:id`

**Given** a mouse or touch user
**When** they click or tap a card
**Then** the app navigates to that park's details

**Given** the user returns from details to the list
**When** the list renders again
**Then** focus moves to the card of the park they came from

### Story 1.3: Park details with missing-data handling

As a resident,
I want to see everything known about a park, and a clear note where information is missing,
So that I can decide whether to visit without being misled.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the user opens `/parks/:id`
**When** details render
**Then** an "All parks" back control appears first, then the photo area, the park name as the view's single `<h1>`, the address, an "Open in OpenStreetMap" link from lat/lng, fact tiles in a `<dl>` for Hours, Size (acres), and Rating ("4.7 / 5"), the About description, and all amenities as checked tags
**And** focus moves to the details heading when details open

**Given** a park with missing data
**When** details render
**Then** each case follows the `design/README.md` missing-data table:
- missing `location.address` (Highland Dog Park) → "Address not listed"
- `description: null` (Old Mill Botanical Garden) → "No description has been provided for this park."
- `rating: null` (Cedar Hill) → "Not yet rated", never 0 or empty stars
- `images: []` (Cedar Hill, East Ridge) → a dashed "No photos for this park yet" box
- missing `hours` or `acreage` → "Not listed"
- missing or empty `amenities` → the section is hidden or shows "No amenities listed"
- missing lat/lng → no OpenStreetMap link
**And** missing-value fact tiles use the muted style (missing background, dashed border, italic text)

**Given** an image fails to load
**When** the `<img>` fires `onError`
**Then** the no-photo box replaces it, with no broken-image icon

**Given** a park has images
**When** details render
**Then** the first image shows with a "Photo 1 of N" label and alt text naming the park

**Given** amenity tokens such as `dog-run`
**When** they render
**Then** they display as readable labels ("Dog run"), and unknown tokens are humanized the same way, never dropped

### Story 1.4: Map of parks synced with selection

As a resident,
I want to see where parks are on a map and open a park from its marker,
So that I can choose by location.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the app loads without location permission
**When** the map renders
**Then** it shows OpenStreetMap tiles via react-leaflet with visible attribution, fitted to the bounds of parks that have coordinates
**And** it never requests the user's location

**Given** parks with valid lat/lng
**When** the map renders
**Then** each has a marker showing its list number, focusable, with the accessible name "N. Park name"
**And** a park with no valid coordinates has no marker but stays in the list
**And** markers render correctly under Vite (no broken default icon)

**Given** desktop width (≥ 768px)
**When** the user activates a marker by click or keyboard
**Then** the app navigates to `/parks/:id` and the sidebar shows that park's details

**Given** a park is selected from either the list or the map
**When** the map renders
**Then** that park's marker is amber and larger than the others

**Given** phone width (< 768px)
**When** the user taps a marker on the Map tab
**Then** a preview card shows "Park N of 12", the name, the address, the hours, a "View park details" button, and a close button labelled "Close park preview"

**Given** map controls
**When** they render
**Then** zoom and close buttons have `aria-label`s and touch targets of at least 44×44px

### Story 1.5: Responsive and accessibility polish with design applied

As a resident on a phone or using a keyboard or screen reader,
I want the app to match the designed layouts and work well in every mode,
So that the main tasks are easy everywhere.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** the screens in `design/screens/`
**When** the app is compared against them at 1280×800 and 390×844
**Then** the layout, tokens, type sizes, corner radius, and spacing match `design/README.md`, except where the SPEC overrides the design

**Given** desktop width (≥ 768px)
**When** the app renders
**Then** a green header shows the app name, subtitle, and park count, with a ~400px independently scrolling sidebar and the map filling the rest

**Given** phone width (< 768px)
**When** the app renders
**Then** a single column shows a List / Map toggle of two `<button>`s with `aria-pressed`, List by default
**And** details take the full screen with an "All parks" back link in the header

**Given** widths of 375px and 1280px
**When** the user completes list → details → back
**Then** there is no horizontal scroll and every interactive element shows the amber focus-visible ring

**Given** the whole app
**When** it is checked for accessibility
**Then** there are no clickable `<div>`s, there is one `<h1>` per view, every icon-only button has an `aria-label`, touch targets are at least 44×44px, and text contrast is at least 4.5:1

### Story 1.6: Tests and submission README

As the interviewer,
I want evidence the app was checked and a README I can run and judge from,
So that I can evaluate usefulness, decisions, and verification.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Given** Vitest + React Testing Library
**When** `npm test` runs
**Then** tests pass that cover every known gap in `park-data.md`: Old Mill's null description, Cedar Hill's empty images and null rating, East Ridge's empty images, Highland's missing address, an image that fails to load, and an unmatched image URL
**And** a test covers the keyboard flow: Tab to a list card, Enter, the details heading receives focus, and "All parks" returns focus to the originating card
**And** a test covers the image URL transformation without mutating the source data

**Given** the README
**When** the interviewer reads it
